the Rake","volume":"婚后外传：公爵在乡村喜剧里破产","anchor":"chapter-0225","paragraphs":["The play began with a model boat blocking the imaginary courthouse. The programmer had given its piston enough power to move, and the Russian postgraduate had forgotten to reduce it. The retired pilot caught it before its rudder could pierce a paper wall. Its pivot survived; the play's dignity did not.","The sound setter solved one procedural difficulty by sitting on the switch. Mara was a little worried about his trousers, but he assured her the box was cold. A settler character had been cut from the revised play, whose plot no longer needed him. Regarding that revision, the novelist offered no apology.","A boy shouted for his mom. His little sister answered, “Mommy is the president.” She was correct. The president had stepped in as the missing judge, leaving the republican bookseller to hold the script. The visiting MP and senator sat side by side on a bench that creaked ominously. The real policeman and policewoman stood behind them, ready to save democracy from the furniture.","Mara entered as the peasant farmer. Cassian followed as the wealthy rake. Their rogue realtor, played by the postman, had sold the farmer's house to the fictional plaintiff, a missionary's offspring with a fondness for unsuitable bargains. The retired prosecutor appeared as a prophet and warned everyone that greed would sear the soul. He delivered the warning in a splendid baritone. The musician gave him one ominous chord.","The screener had become a proud payer himself, buying a ticket after his shift. He regarded the dog's free entrance miserably until it stole a spoon; then he decided employment was preferable.","The novelist's dialogue was ridiculous. The poet's songs were worse. The audience adored both. A refugee student played the realtor's rival; the orphan actor played a ranger who refused to remove the farmer. The nonconformist minister carried a sign reading ONWARD TO THE APPEAL, which had been made from a recruitment poster. The recruiter watched with approval.","A young pupil stood beside the poet, holding the pages that threatened to fall. The elderly truth seeker in the front row applauded the worst rhyme. His grandson called him Pop and begged for mercy. A pie-loving omnivore could apparently tolerate a great deal once dessert had arrived.","The salesperson had become a playgoer, sitting beside a passer-by who had entered to escape the rain. A seed saver and a fruit picker shared the last pie. The dog sitter restrained a dog that wanted to join the cast. Its owner, a sensitive school pupil, warned it not to eat the costume. The dog had no respect for scholastic effort.","At the back, the museum volunteer watched the archive case. Earlier a viewer had asked about “Negro” in the old missionary leaflet. He explained its historical use and why it was not a respectful present-day term. The map's “Oriental” label received the same contextual care. A queer teenager and a religious grandparent listened together, then returned to the play. Their conversation needed no stage applause.","A purist grumbled that the text had changed. The novelist admitted it freely. The revision was meant to symbolize the farmer's choice, not imprison the actors in a bad first draft. A secular choir member laughed; the nonconformist minister quietly gave her his place nearer the stage. The barn had become a temporary community rather than an argument over membership.","The pilot examined the speaker analytically, then took a new battery from his pocket. A slippery loose cable had made the signal fail. He could sort out that problem without another speech about standards. In response to the renewed music, the uninvited dog rose and attempted a dance of its own.","A seasonal gardener entered with a rake, which was not the rake the script required. Cassian accepted the tool and tried to continue. The visiting peer lost his composure. So did the Polynesian navigator, who had spent the afternoon patiently teaching children to steer and now found himself unable to breathe for laughter.","The farmer refused the realtor's contract. The renter would keep her house; the plaintiff would receive her money back. Their fictional settlement had no bearing on the tax code, the narrator warned. It did, however, permit a dance. The president refused to let the populist guest turn it into a campaign speech.","The powerhouse musician began the final tune. The World Cup tournament screen went dark behind her, its operator pressing mute at exactly the wrong time. The play's fictional prophet had predicted ruin. He received a kiss from his wife instead. The puritan rushed past in a missing shoe.","Cassian offered Mara his hand. He had lost his hat again. She took it, looked at the revised timescale and decided the play could run late. Their dance would not penalize anyone. The smile he gave her suggested he might make a career of playing an impoverished rogue if she continued choosing him at the end.","'Sir, the next cue is yours,' the president hissed. Cassian had got stuck on the sight of his wife laughing. The delay was regrettable for the plot and pleasant for him. One should enjoy oneself while one could. Mara took his hand and allowed that explanation to stand."],"questions":[],"wordCount":866,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0225\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div The whale was the convention's prize, borrowed from a museum for its costume contest. Mara had won the honour of transporting it by being the last person to say no. She promised to see off the next organizer who called that democratic selection.","Their carriage held a former soldier, a sociologist and a steelworker who had travelled together for years. A unionist wanted to unionize the unpaid event volunteers. A stockbroker offered to analyze the refreshments budget. A statistician pointed out that the proposed sample size was one sandwich. The holiday had barely begun.","A superstar at a local theatre occupied the next seat. Her beloved spouse was an auctioneer dressed as an alien, and their son had fallen asleep against his mask. The unlikely sleeper made Mara smile. A voter who recognized the actress asked for a photograph; she agreed after he promised not to wake the child.","A toddler wearing tiger ears claimed the whale. His father, a teamster, apologized. His spouse had taken their son to find the dining car, but the child had found a better destination. Cassian moved the trophy to a higher shelf. A wasp buzzed against the window. Mara opened the vent, and the insect took the opportunity to leave.","The next compartment belonged to an actress playing the Virgin Queen. Her stepdaughter wore a witch's hat and had brought a plush monster named Spook. A tutor tried to maintain reading time. The child, an energetic talker, wanted the queen to name a successor. “My super powers are limited,” the actress told her. “Ask the sovereign after lunch.”","The little witch had a plan to bring up everyone's spirits. Her queen would give a speech, and the spymaster would steal her crown. The instructor tried to misguide them into a quieter game; the children were not inclined to accept. A journalistic fan offered to work through the script instead. Mara thought the joint rehearsal might be worth trying.","A sportsman in a Superman costume had got lost between cars. A top executive dressed as a spy offered directions. A former taxpayer adviser, now a subscriber to every detective journal imaginable, declared him a suspicious character. His spymaster, he explained, was the little girl with the witch's hat. The roles had already become more interesting than the planned contest.","The safety tester, a self-described thinker, had no patience for a would-be traitor blocking the passage. He meant the actor in a villain's coat. A theatrical upstart might enjoy attention, but his subordinate, dressed as a trapper, needed room to carry a case. Mara helped them move it. She had begun to sympathise with Vera.","The event's strategist was a workaholic named Vera. She was running an open-access fan archive as well as the convention. Her peninsular hometown expected an enormous parade. Worldwide entries had arrived, including a costume from a transcendentalist poet's admirer and a travel diary labelled THE SOJOURNER. She had worked tirelessly to catalogue them. Regrettably, she had not eaten.","A school voter asked whether he could choose the contest winner. Vera explained the system. The child found it weird but accepted that his favourite alien might not win. His mom called the costume undesirable; he called it super. She declined to sue anyone over the disagreement. A taker of risks had been born.","Mara gave her a sandwich. The safety tester came to check the luggage rack. He had been told to admire the trophy regardless of its weight. Procedurally, he preferred to secure it first. The volunteer beside him called the repeated inspections soul-crushingly tedious. Mara thought a train full of children was worth a little patience.","They passed a heritage display about the fictional port's early connection to the slave trade. A local activist had insisted its story remain visible. An advertiser had wanted only pretty railway pictures; the museum's American contributor disagreed. History should not be opaque merely because a holiday brochure looked better without it.","The visiting Australian performer and a westerner from a desert town were studying an old trapper's diary. The latter had been a transient traveler, the caption said. A British traveller beside them argued that the spelling should follow the original. Both could read the page. Their disagreement did not erode the pleasure of the story.","A smoker stepped out to a designated area at the station. A watcher on the platform saw a tramp in an old coat exchange bags with a man dressed as a terrorist from a bad spy film. They turned out to be actors. The guard asked them to remove the alarming props before boarding. No costume entitled them to spook strangers without warning.","The train jolted. Cassian steadied the whale. Mara steadied Cassian. At the end of the corridor, Vera checked her smartphone, went pale and announced that the contest's rival trophy had vanished. The golden tiger was missing, and the next station was twenty miles away.","Vera looked at her smartphone. The organizer feared an undesired argument might develop into a scandal. She should analyze the records before she let anxiety erode her judgment. Cassian suggested they press on with the search. Commonly, a misplaced object was less dramatic than a stolen one. A quick check would be worth making."],"questions":[],"wordCount":888,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0226\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：卧铺列车的金鲸鱼</div><h1>The Sleeper Car Wakes Up</h1><p id=\"chapter-0226-p1\">Cassian <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1021\" data-words=\"1021\">boarded</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-7638\" data-words=\"7638\">sleeper</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-8616\" data-words=\"8616\">train</a> carrying <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> suitcase, <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> bouquet <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-383\" data-words=\"383\">and</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3646\" data-words=\"3646\">golden</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-9190\" data-words=\"9190\">whale</a>. <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">The</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-9190\" data-words=\"9190\">whale</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-792\" data-words=\"792\">was</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-8385\" data-words=\"8385\">the</a> convention&#x27;s <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-6470\" data-words=\"6470\">prize</a>, <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1054\" data-words=\"1054\">borrowed</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3450\" data-words=\"3450\">from</a> <a class=\"vocab\" href=\"#word-1\" data-words=\"1\">a</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-5390\" data-words=\"5390\">museum</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-3344\" data-words=\"3344\">for</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-4571\" data-words=\"4571,4568\">its</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1935\" data-words=\"1935\">costume</a> <a class=\"vocab first\" href=\"#word-1832\" data-words=\"1832\">contest</a>. Her stepmother, the actress dressed as the Virgin Queen, gave royal approval. Her stepdaughter's enthusiasm was not limited by constitutional detail. The tutor surrendered the reading book and asked only that the monster Spook remain out of the soup.","The royal character was a sovereign only until teatime. Her actual stepdaughter accepted that limit. A visiting superstar applauded the compromise, while the troupe's transient sojourner found his seat. He had been promoted from trapper to court guard. The move seemed super to him, though it left a spare fur hat on the table.","Cassian followed carrying the golden whale, which had become inseparable from his reputation. The sportsman in the Superman costume offered to take it. A steelworker checked his grip before agreeing. Nobody should turn a costume contest into a test of whether a toddler could catch heavy metal. The toddler, still wearing tiger ears, objected strongly.","Vera wanted to sue the carrier. The soldier suggested they look first. The sociologist asked whether anyone had actually seen the golden tiger on the train. Their statistician admitted that a missing item could have remained at the station. The stockbroker advised against treating every loss as a conspiracy. His advice startled everyone, including him.","The talker in the witch hat had found a quiet sleeper behind the curtain. She promised not to wake her. Vera admired this weird restraint and nominated the child her successor. The little girl refused an undesirable promotion that involved paperwork. Mara thought the refusal might be worth trying.","The strategist had treated every missed message as proof of her own failure. A subordinate brought the original delivery receipt. Its words could not misguide anyone who read them slowly, though the handwriting was poor. Procedurally, the carrier had asked for a signature before packing. Regardless of everyone's memory, that record was the useful starting point.","The workaholic organizer finally sat down. She had worked tirelessly, and the opaque parcel system had confused her. The shipping form used the keyword CAT for both a costume and the trophy. An advertiser wanted a photo immediately. The open-access archive would have to wait. Mara helped Vera work through the original entries rather than the filtered list.","A visiting thinker proposed that the tiger had symbolically escaped. A practical teamster proposed checking the baggage van. The unionist offered help, though he still intended to unionize the volunteers next month. A sports photographer said he was a taker of pictures, not responsibility. His joke met silence. He picked up a bag instead.","Their safety tester, an affluent passenger who had volunteered his skills, checked the storage area. A westerner from the desert helped hold the door. An undesired rattle came from an unsecured solar lamp. They fixed its strap before continuing. Regrettably, the people selling paid media coverage on the platform had already advertised a missing treasure.","A son looking for his father stopped beside the heritage display. Its account of the slave trade did not fit his expectation of a pretty old train. The activist answered his questions without demanding gratitude. A local voter joined them. Her ancestor had worked at the docks after emancipation. She wanted the railway remembered with all its passengers, not just the wealthy ones.","The smoker returned with a rumor. A watcher had seen the man in the tramp costume carrying a striped package. His fictional spymaster had apparently ordered him to hide it. The top executive in the spy coat denied involvement with considerable theatrical pleasure. Mara gave him a look. He confessed to hiding sandwiches, which he surrendered in return for silence.","The film's terrorist was now making tea. His supposed traitor had become the carrier of sugar. Nobody would let the costumes get the best of ordinary manners. A fan of the transcendentalist poet read aloud from a fictional diary, and the atmosphere grew calm enough to hear the tutor's next question.","The peninsular landscape slid past. An Australian traveller pointed out birds; an American traveler tried to photograph a wasp on the glass. Their commonly misspelt names had been printed twice on the guest list. Vera promised to correct them. She did not want a typo to develop into an argument about who was welcome.","Vera hoped to see off the worst rumours before the next stop. The sponsor had wanted a paid media moment; he could have a correct one. Cassian reminded her that checking fire safety and luggage straps was not less important because it looked tedious. She could sympathise with disappointment without cancelling those checks.","The former taxpayer adviser, an affluent subscriber to the archive, had opened the wrong case. Inside was a papier-mâché sovereign's head. He replaced it and hoped nobody had noticed. The little witch had. She called him an upstart and demanded allegiance. His spouse laughed until he apologized.","Mara found the golden tiger in a crate marked SOLAR LANTERNS. A defective handle had required repacking. The volunteer who did it had left a clear note, but the note was under the inventory. The show was not a worldwide crime network after all. It was an exhausted person trying to help.","Vera looked soul-crushingly embarrassed. Cassian refused that verdict. “A note was missed. We found it.”","The little witch, however, had another announcement. The golden whale had moved while everyone was looking for the tiger. A rogue superhero had borrowed it to hold down a map. A toddler with tiger ears demanded a second crown. His father, the teamster, negotiated with another biscuit.","The queen's most loyal taker of orders was an auctioneer in an alien mask. His son woke, saw him and asked whether his ancestor had looked like that. His spouse said probably not. The talker in the witch costume declared the answer inconclusive. Everyone accepted her authority for the next three minutes.","The unionist asked Vera to consider paid shifts for next year. If volunteers chose to unionize, she would not treat them as undesirable. Their work had kept the worldwide convention moving. The steelworker nodded. A top executive could learn something from a person who knew when to put a tool down. The workaholic organizer accepted the rebuke more easily after lunch.","The sociologist had become the table's thinker. Her statistician friend wanted to know whether the extra cake had improved everyone's mood. The stockbroker argued that the sample remained small. The taxpayer adviser, a faithful subscriber, offered to sponsor a larger study if he received a slice. Nobody mistook the exchange for actual research.","An affluent voter asked the sovereign for an autograph. The actress agreed in return for a promise to stop calling her royalty. The upstart with the monster Spook watched gravely. She would not let disappointment erode her dignity. Cassian tried to analyze her expression and received a severe look for his trouble.","The sleeper carriage would be cleaned at the resort. A subordinate asked the strategist whether to leave the sovereign's head aboard. Vera said no. Her successor could begin training by checking the case. It was super to have help, she admitted. The morning's desire to sue the railway was beginning to seem excessive.","Mara walked back to the heritage case with the little girl. She wanted to see the model ship associated with the port's slave trade. Her mother explained why its beautiful workmanship did not make its history beautiful. The child listened, then asked why the museum kept it. The activist said that remembering could include uncomfortable objects, provided people explained them honestly.","The watcher at the next window lowered his camera. His journalistic instincts had almost made him photograph the family without asking. The Australian traveller told him to stop. The American traveler agreed, although their perennial spelling rivalry resumed over a note for the archive. The British tutor suggested letting the original author choose. This seemed an excellent way to end a very small war.","The westerner joined the fan of the transcendentalist writer for tea. Their superstar companion had removed her mask and become another tired passenger. The actor in the terrorist costume promised not to wear it at the parade. He would be an imaginary traitor in a plain coat instead. The distinction did not help the photographer, who wanted recognizable villains.","The smoker had left the platform for the final time. A transient sojourner in a long coat joined the dining car, carrying a borrowed hat. He had been cast as a tramp, then promoted to trapper because he owned suitable boots. A fan of the transcendentalist writer wanted his picture. He asked whether the costume was historically convincing. She said it was weird, and meant that kindly.","The peninsular resort came into view. Vera had worked tirelessly to make their arrival perfect. Now she hoped merely to see off the travelers with their own luggage. Procedurally, the guard wanted a count. Regardless of the delayed parade, every child and adult would be accounted for. The safety tester handled this with more patience than any superhero costume had inspired.","Cassian carried the whale to the van. Mara followed with the tiger. A wasp investigated the flowers and was gently redirected by the ranger at the station. Vera said the trip had been soul-crushingly complicated. Mara disagreed. She would sympathise with tiredness, but the journey had contained too much kindness to be only that.","The workaholic's smartphone rang again. She let it wait. One could press on and still pause long enough to drink. The habit of treating every message as urgent would get the best of her if she let it. Cassian offered the borrowed whale an undignified pat. Mara told him to leave the museum's beloved treasure alone.","The sportsman, the unionist and the child all agreed to help with the next year. The open-access archive acquired three new contributors. A former subordinate asked Vera to train a successor so she could rest. The proposal met no objection. The golden trophies had started a conversation worth having, even if nobody had intended it.","When the platform emptied, Cassian offered Mara the untouched bouquet. “Finally.”","She took it and pulled him close by the lapel. “You used to do that before involving a whale.”","He kissed her. Tart","volume":"婚后外传：红宝石与覆盆子挞","anchor":"chapter-0229","paragraphs":["Mara's ruby necklace matched the raspberry tart Cassian stole from the chef's tray. The chef noticed. He was an old friend and demanded payment in the form of carrying six trays to the terrace. The chief executive of a formidable company obeyed, while his wife enjoyed the sight.","The masked supper brought together an improbable group. Its hostess, a retired diplomat, wanted guests to cook together before dancing. A champion swimmer found himself paired with a contemplative clergyman. A cheerleader showed a classicist how to roll pastry. Their coach had never supervised a kitchen and was enjoying his helplessness. The comic hired for the evening offered advice until the chef gave him an apron.","The chef had promised to judge every competitor as an equal. He would see the meal as a joint creation, not a contest between social ranks. The local inhabitant beside a visiting emissary found that reassuring. The envoy, an enthusiastic cook, immediately burned a finger and called himself an idiot. The chef handed him a cold cloth without laughing.","A bilingual Briton translated instructions for an immigrant guest who understood everything except the names of utensils. A contributor to the local museum found an old recipe in a medieval annal. Its Celsius conversion was badly calculated. An economist and a geneticist argued over the result. Mara suggested asking the person who used an oven every day.","Cassian listened to a Catholic elder explaining the recipe's history. The man's wife, universally called Granny, interrupted to say he had never successfully baked it. A grandparent who misremembered domestic facts could survive correction. A prestigious reputation should not make anyone immune to being wrong. The couple's affectionate argument left Mara hopeful about growing old.","The hotel's executive director looked worn out. A teenage instructor had brought apprentices to help, and she wanted to give them her undivided attention. Their developmental training mattered more than another polished speech. An environmentalist asked how the kitchen regulated food waste. The chef showed him. His systematic approach used processed ingredients sparingly, without pretending fresh produce could never spoil.","A headhunter offered the instructor a new job. She asked him to defer the conversation. The developer beside him wanted to discuss a new restaurant, and a generalist consultant claimed he could evaluate any site. The geographer suggested they visit one first. A freelance correspondent wrote the exchange down, hoping for a funny column rather than a row.","Near the fountain, a federal museum visitor showed a historian a tiny fossil. The historian knew more about manuscripts. A local explorer had found the stone and wanted to know whether it belonged to a protected site. Mara liked the frank admission that none of them could decide from one glance. A gentleman could be helpful without pretending expertise.","The clock enthusiast wondered whether the old mechanism might be handy in timing the sauce. Mara advised a modern watch. A sophisticated clock might look divine and still run slowly. Its failure could cripple supper if everyone waited for its bell. Cassian offered to smuggle her a tart if the delay became serious. She would be worse off only if he ate it first.","Their host's benefactor, an idealist with a titanic laugh, invited everyone to unite over supper. A Democrat and a conservative cynic sat together. A controversialist tried to start an argument about food politics, but the cashier from the village bakery asked him to pass the butter. The small request defeated the grand strategy.","The setting was beautifully arranged. A high-backed chair allowed a local diner to sit comfortably beside her friends. It was not an inferior substitute for a place on the terrace; the table had simply been moved to meet her needs. Mara hoped the hotel would build on that practical success. An increasingly elaborate ceremony was not necessary.","Mara turned to find a guy in a dragon mask blocking the stairs. His infant niece had fallen asleep against his shoulder. He asked for a quieter room. Cassian guided him away. The party could be joyful without making a baby captive to its noise. Nobody would oppress a tired family with compulsory fun.","A retired headmaster had come in search of a missing recipe card. His former pupil, now a chef, offered a first-class substitute. An enthusiast for antique clocks tried to divine where the original might have gone. His guesses were entertaining and useless. The dining room was fully operational; its dessert order did not require supernatural assistance.","“Darling,” Cassian said, returning, “you look dangerously pleased.”","She smiled. “I perceive six trays have improved you.”","He offered a painless way to test the claim: a dance. Regardless of the noise, he could make a little room for her. She took his hand. Their motion caught the ruby's light, and a woman in a silver mask looked away too quickly.","Then the lights failed. Somebody brushed Mara's shoulder. When they returned, her necklace was gone, and a child was shouting from behind the service wall."],"questions":[],"wordCount":832,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0229\"
